# Platform Build Log

Append-only log of work sessions. Each session adds exactly one row, in two edits:

1. **At session start** (before any real work): append a row with status `⏳ IN FLIGHT — {step name}`, commit it immediately as its own standalone commit.
2. **At session end** (after the real work is done, tested, and typechecked): update that same row to `✅ DONE — {step name}`, filling in the real commit hash of the work, then commit the update (either folded into the final commit or immediately after it).

If a session dies, crashes, or is abandoned mid-way, its row stays `⏳ IN FLIGHT`, which is itself the record that an attempt was made and didn't finish.

See [CLAUDE.md](CLAUDE.md) for the exact instructions.

History before 2026-08-12 lives in [PLATFORM_BUILD_ARCHIVE_2026-08-12.md](PLATFORM_BUILD_ARCHIVE_2026-08-12.md) (Git #948). Pull that file for prior sessions' landed implementations, gotchas, and decisions.

| 2026-08-21 | ✅ DONE | Customer Portal v2 -- **Risk Register + Policy Decisions wired to REAL data** (commit `2d0703ad`, follow-up `ac85f5f0`). Both pages rendered the design fixture; they now render the calling customer's own rows from `msp_risk_decisions`. RECON CHANGED THE SHAPE OF THE JOB: the real table is `msp_risk_decisions` and its ONLY route was `msp-rbd.ts`, entirely MSP-side (`MSPOperator` to list, `MSPAdmin` to sign/revoke, scoped by `resolveMspIdStrict`) -- no customer-scoped read and **no customer-scoped write**. The typed-name + checkbox acceptance existed as UI ONLY: `AcceptRiskPanel`'s `onConfirm` set local React state and opened a form drawer that persisted nothing, so THE ACCEPTANCE WRITE PATH HAD TO BE BUILT, not merely wired. New `portal-risk-register.ts`, `requireRole("CustomerUser")` (a deliberately HIGHER floor than the `Assessment` used by change-control/remediation-tracker -- this page carries dollar liability and a signature surface), scoped via the shared `resolveTenantScope` (JWT customerId -> tenants row -> mspId + tenantId, BOTH as predicates, failing closed on a blank identifier): `GET /api/portal/risk-register`, `GET /api/portal/policy-decisions`, `POST /api/portal/risk-register/:rbdId/accept`. THE ACCEPTANCE IS PERMANENT: any accept on a row already carrying `accepted_at` is refused 409, and the UPDATE repeats `accepted_at IS NULL` as an atomic race guard so two tabs cannot both sign; `acceptedAt`/`ipAddress`/`signatureHash` are derived SERVER-side, unlike `msp-rbd.ts`'s sign handler which takes ip and hash from the request body; the exact ticked sentence is snapshotted so a later reword cannot rewrite what was agreed. THE TABLE DID NOT HOLD WHAT THESE PAGES RENDER -- no pillar, owner, likelihood, impact, weight, outcome, evidence, plan, register ref, rationale, obligation (WIRING_PLAN.md predicted exactly this); added as 18 nullable columns, no backfill, and NULL IS SERVED AS NULL ("Not recorded") because a fabricated likelihood would put a real risk on the heat map at coordinates nobody chose. FOUND AND FIXED A REAL DEFECT via its own test: acceptance dates formatted in the viewer's LOCAL timezone, so a signature stamped at midnight UTC read as the previous day west of Greenwich -- the same permanent record showing two different dates depending on who opened it; pinned to UTC with a regression test on both ends of the UTC day. GATES: tsc clean (0 errors in every file touched; 76 api-server + 1 portal errors are pre-existing in files I did not touch), msp-portal 1533/1533, 12 new route tests + 17 new normalisation tests all green, both builds clean. MANIFESTS RUN GREEN against the deployed server: portal-v2-risk-register 46/46 TWICE (proving re-runnability), portal-v2-policy-decisions 33/33 (its first run was 25/30 and caught two manifest mistakes, not page bugs). The successful write was proven separately end-to-end -- RBD-2026-103 accepted for real, verified in the DB, second accept refused 409, seed re-run to restore it. SEEDED TEST DATA, SAID PLAINLY: the testbed tenant had no risk rows (the table's one row is `contoso-01`, matching no real tenant), so a seed migration adds four for tenant `c4c814d4`; the read/scope/write paths are real, the row content is demonstration data. **SHANE TO-DO:** the recorded `ipAddress` came back `127.0.0.1` -- the app is behind Replit's proxy with Express `trust proxy` unset, so `req.ip` is the proxy hop, not the customer's address, and that audit field is not meaningful until you set it (do NOT "fix" it by trusting `x-forwarded-for`, which is spoofable without it -- it is an app-wide setting, so it is your call). | `2d0703ad` |

| 2026-08-21 | ✅ DONE | Customer Portal v2 — **SOPs & Runbooks wired to real data** (`/portal-v2/sops` + its library / execution queue / execution history sub-views). Part 6 built this page against the design's fixtures in `sopHubData.ts`; this session replaced them with real, customer-scoped data without touching layout or structure. NEW `artifacts/api-server/src/routes/portal-sops.ts` serving `GET /api/portal/sops` and `GET /api/portal/sop-runs`, registered in `routes/index.ts` (2 lines, landed alongside a concurrent session's `portalOwnershipRouter` line without conflict). SCOPING NEEDED BOTH SHAPES, and getting it wrong is how a cross-tenant leak happens: the LIBRARY is `msp_sops`, keyed on `msp_id` only, so a customer sees the procedure library their own MSP maintains; the RUNS are `msp_sop_runs`, keyed on `(msp_id, tenant_id)` where `tenant_id` is the free-text M365 identifier, so BOTH predicates apply via `resolveTenantScope`, which fails closed rather than returning a partial scope (a blank `tenantId` would make `eq(tenantId,'')` match every other blank-identifier row). No route reads an id from a query param, body or path segment — the JWT is the only thing that selects rows. READ-ONLY ON PURPOSE: a customer-facing POST would let the portal start a real procedure against the tenant without passing the change-control gate, which is the one thing that gate exists to prevent. ROLE FLOOR DELIBERATELY HIGHER THAN ITS SIBLINGS: `requireRole("CustomerUser")` where `portal-runbooks.ts` / `portal-change-control.ts` / `portal-remediation-tracker.ts` all sit at `Assessment` — flagged rather than silently matched, and the manifest pins the testbed account's role so a future move to an Assessment-tier account fails loudly with the real role in the failure text instead of every UI step failing unexplained. (A stale note on an older manifest claimed `TEST_PORTAL_EMAIL` was Assessment-role; verified live — it is CustomerUser, customerId 1.) THE HONEST-MAPPING RULE, in `lib/portal-sops.ts` and unit-tested: a derived value must be TRUE of the row it came from. `level`/`runnable`/`mode`/`who runs it` derive from `automation_type` AND from whether a step really carries a `graphEndpoint` — a row typed `hybrid` with no endpoint is NOT runnable, because the library's Runnable flag would otherwise be a lie. Review cadence is stored nowhere, so it reads "Not recorded" rather than being guessed from `version_status`. The audit view's hash is a REAL SHA-256 over the entry's own fields in the design's `a91f…7c02` form, because the copy on screen promises "every entry is hashed" — a random-looking string would have rendered identically and meant nothing. Category and compliance-tag filter vocabularies are built from the tenant's own rows (the prototype's fixed lists named the prototype's sample values and would have offered a customer filters matching nothing); execution-type and source stay fixed design vocabulary. The hold banner now reads the REAL hold summary off `/api/portal/runbooks` — which is what the fixture it replaces said in as many words a later pass should do — so it and Active Runbooks can no longer disagree. PORTAL SIDE: `sopHubData.ts` DROPPED `SOP_LIBRARY`/`SOP_META`/`SOP_QUEUE`/`SOP_AUDIT`/`SOP_CAT_OPTIONS`/`SOP_TAGS`/`SOP_HOLD_BANNER` and the nine-person RACI map (a fixture nobody imports is still one somebody can re-import), keeping design constants and wire types; owner avatars now carry the REAL person the row names — a SOP's last editor, a run's operator — resolved server-side so library and queue cannot disagree about who someone is. New `useSops.ts` hook; every derivation in `sopHubModel.ts` now takes the payload as an argument instead of reading fixtures at module scope, and gained the three cases a fixture could never produce and real data does daily: empty library (`resolveSelectedId`/`detailFor` return null rather than indexing `[0]` of nothing), zero total (`statCards` no longer divides by zero into "NaN%"), and no open hold windows (banner absent, not "0 running"). THE FOUR CATEGORY PAGES ARE THE ONE PART DELIBERATELY UNCHANGED, with the reason recorded in the file: re-verified against the design source rather than trusting the prior session's comment — `sop-incident-response`/`sop-security-drift`/`sop-mail-flow`/`sop-device-mgmt` occur in `Customer Portal Shell.dc.html` ONLY in the `sopCategories` list (8789-8793), the page-title fallback (19023) and the palette index (19117), with no markup branch, so they carry no tenant data to replace and a fetch would have changed nothing on screen. VERIFIED: msp-portal 1506/1506 green (31 rewritten SOP assertions incl. the new empty/zero cases); api-server `lib/portal-sops.test.ts` 25/25 green and registered in the vitest include allowlist (which is an allowlist — a new .test.ts silently does not run otherwise); msp-portal tsc clean apart from the known pre-existing SowScreen(359,13) TS7030; api-server tsc reports ZERO errors in the three files this touched (its 123 others are pre-existing, all in admin-build-tracker.ts / ai-*.test.ts / sow-claim-binding-560.test.ts); isolated vite build clean in 53s. (2) `early` used `Math.ceil`, so the design's own `hold-guest` fixture (217 hours = 9 days 1 hour) rendered "Close the window **10** days early" where the README's own alerting copy says 9 — floor, plus a whole-day requirement; rounding a deadline UP is the wrong direction anyway. (3) "closes tomorrow" came from `hoursLeft <= 24`, so a 20-hour remainder at 01:00 UTC claimed tomorrow when it closed the SAME DAY — it now compares UTC calendar dates. (4) the badge switched state at 24h while the readout switched units at 48h, pairing "Holding" with "T-30h" — one threshold now. `HOLD_DEFS[].c`, a per-window colour nothing ever read, is not carried over. The clock is LIVE: a 30s tick re-derives from `closesAt` so a window crosses T-24 on an open tab without a reload — the one thing the README explicitly flags as missing from the prototype — and it is a tick, not a poll, because only the scan verdict can move server-side and scans run hourly at best. The client mirror (`holds/holdState.ts`) is a deliberate second copy GUARDED by a drift test pinning the same four cases at the same boundaries as the server's, the same shape `remediation-tracker-catalogue.test.ts` uses across the same app boundary. The nav badge is wired for real — the README calls it "the single place in the nav that says a decision is waiting" — driven by the actual T-0 count, pill when expanded and 6px dot when collapsed. **THE CR GATE IS REAL:** the four hold decisions raise actual change requests and record the link in `portal_hold_window_events.change_request_id`; in the prototype they are `openForm` calls that stop at a confirmation. Releasing deliberately does NOT execute anything or tick the gated step — it raises the CR that asks for the release and closes the window, because collapsing the two would be the portal making a tenant change on a button press. VERIFIED: `tsc` clean on both apps (msp-portal's pre-existing `SowScreen.tsx` TS7030 only; api-server's 76-error baseline unchanged, 0 in my files), `vite build` clean, **32/32** hold-state tests + 28/28 change-control + **19/19** client drift-guard + 17/17 view-model. The close-early path was then exercised LIVE against the deployed server: it returned `CR-2026-138`, which appeared in the customer's own Change Control register as "Close hold window 9 days early — Guest owner confirmation — 14 day window", status Pending approval, carrying `linkedFinding`; the window's audit trail recorded `closed_early` with the reason and the CR code; and a second close was refused 409. **THE MANIFEST EARNED ITS KEEP ON THE FIRST RUN (45/48):** it caught that the standfirst rendered `&rsquo;` where the prototype's own bytes are a straight U+0027 — a real copy-fidelity bug of mine, since copy is final and prettifying typography is rewriting it. The other two failures were MY assertions pinning exact figures on a moving clock ("T-21h", "T-23d") when the seed writes close moments relative to now and minutes elapse mid-run; the code was right and the test was wrong, and both now assert the thing the defect guard is about rather than a number the clock moves. **AN EARLIER RUN CAME BACK 15/49 FROM ONE CAUSE:** the seed step authenticated with `BUILD_TRACKER_INGEST_TOKEN`, which is NOT among BuildConsole's configured Test Environment Variables (confirmed by reading the list), so nothing seeded and everything downstream failed for want of data rather than for a defect. Rather than leave a manifest that only goes green after a settings change somebody has to remember, `/admin/testbed/seed-runbooks` now takes a normal customer session and reads `customerId` from the JWT, IGNORING the body — a SMALLER authority than its neighbours in `admin-testbed.ts`, which take an ingest token because they act across customers; this one cannot point at another tenant at all. | 2026-08-19 | ✅ DONE | Portal v2 Governance hero — drift-trend end-point dot rendered as a stretched oval, now a true circle. Root cause was NOT the dot itself: the hero chart svg in `artifacts/msp-portal/src/pages/portal-v2-governance.tsx` is `preserveAspectRatio="none"` over a `0 0 280 84` viewBox drawn at `width="100%"` — a deliberately anisotropic scale (x by containerWidth/280, y by exactly 1) so the line spans the card's fluid width, and every child inherits that CTM. The `<circle r={3.5}>` end-point dot therefore painted 10.5×7 px in a 420px-wide container and 5.5×7 px at the 220px min-width; the `<polyline>` hid the identical distortion because a line has no aspect ratio to betray and its stroke was already `vectorEffect="non-scaling-stroke"`. Fix moves the dot OUT of the stretched coordinate space into its own sibling overlay svg (absolute `top:0 left:0`, `width="100%"`, `height={trend.h}`, `overflow:visible`, NO viewBox — so 1 user unit = 1 CSS px and r=3.5 is a true 7px circle at every width), with `cx={`${(trend.lastX / trend.w) * 100}%`}` derived from `govTrendGeometry()` so it still lands exactly where it did (lastX=280 → 100%, cy=67.2px unchanged — the vertical axis was never distorted, scaleY was exactly 1, so size and position are visually identical apart from no longer being stretched). The trend line's shape, the chart width and the card dimensions are untouched, per the brief. Prototype-fidelity note: `Design/design_handoff_customer_portal/Customer Portal Shell.dc.html` line 436 carries this same defect in all six pillar heroes (gov/sec/hlt/adp/lic/cmp) — the transcription was faithful, the design source was wrong; governance is the only one transcribed to React so far. `artifacts/msp-portal/src/components/governance/PolicyDriftTrend.tsx` (a separate, older component, not portal-v2) has the same class of defect at its own line 76 — left alone, out of scope, flagged for Shane. NO TEST MANIFEST, deliberately: the element is `aria-hidden` decoration and circle-vs-oval is a rendered-geometry property that no manifest primitive (expect `state` / `textContains`) can assert — a presence-only assertion would misrepresent the coverage. | 2026-08-13 | ✅ DONE | BuildConsole (WPF, Epic #803) — `UiTestExecutor.ReadResponseBodyAsync` content-stream read hang guard. Symptom class: after a step's captureResponse wait resolves with a matched response, the body is read via `args.Response.GetContentAsync()` then `new StreamReader(stream).ReadToEndAsync()` — both `await`ed UNGUARDED, so a WebView2 content stream that opens but never finishes delivering (or `GetContentAsync` that never returns) deadlocks the run with no further log output, exactly like the #803 screenshot/captureResponse hangs. **Checked the follow-up the task called out: yes, `ReadToEndAsync()` (the line right after `GetContentAsync`) was equally unguarded — so BOTH awaits are now wrapped**, not just the first. Each is guarded with the same `Task.WhenAny(work, Task.Delay(ResponseBodyReadTimeoutMs))` shape navigation (15s)/captureResponse (5s)/screenshot (#995, 8s) already use; new `ResponseBodyReadTimeoutMs = 5000` (scaled to captureResponse's 5s since it reads that same captured response, inside the task's 5-8s range). On either timeout the method returns a clear `(null, "GetContentAsync timed out after 5000ms")` / `(null, "ReadToEndAsync timed out after 5000ms")` result — the caller (`_ , bodyReadError) = await ReadResponseBodyAsync(...)` at the post-capture path) surfaces it as a body-read failure and the step completes instead of the run awaiting indefinitely. Orphaned-task safety: on the GetContentAsync-timeout path a late/never stream is disposed + its fault observed in a `ContinueWith`; on the ReadToEndAsync-timeout path the in-flight read owns reader+stream disposal via a `ContinueWith` (never torn out from under a pending read), so no leak and no unobserved-task exception. | 2026-08-13 | ✅ DONE | BuildConsole (WPF, Git #980) — Build Watch panel: `SlotGrid.Rows/Columns` are now computed from the real current count of active (occupied) slots and re-applied on every occupancy change instead of the fixed 2x4. New `ReflowSlotGrid()` (called from the constructor, `OccupySlot`, and `ClearSlot`) maps the occupied-slot count → grid dims: 1→1x1 (a single build fills the whole window), 2→1x2, 3-4→2x2, 5-6→2x3, 7-8→2x4 (the true max); 0 collapses to 1x1 (full-window empty state) and any overflow clamps to 2x4. Because a `UniformGrid` lays children row-major across exactly Rows×Columns cells and a slot can be freed at any index (dismiss / auto-evict / issue-close), `ReflowSlotGrid` also reorders the child collection to pack occupied slots to the front in stable `_slots` order — so every live build stays on-screen regardless of its physical slot index while the empty slots fall outside the shrunk grid. Idempotent (only mutates the child collection when the desired order actually differs). No logging change needed (pure UI layout; slot occupancy already logs to the `build-watch` channel). No DB. | 2026-08-13 | ✅ DONE | BuildConsole (WPF, Epic #803) — `UiTestExecutor` post-capture-timeout hang fix. Symptom: a uiSteps manifest (e.g. #958 `hello-world-ui.json`) navigates fine (`NAV OK` logged), the `/api/public/hero-headlines` captureResponse hits its own 5s `Task.WhenAny` timeout with `capturedArgs == null` (an EXPECTED valid case), then the run hangs for minutes with no further log output. Root cause: the only `await` remaining in that null path is the always-on #977 screenshot capture — `CaptureScreenshotAsync` → `CoreWebView2.CapturePreviewAsync` — which is `await`ed UNGUARDED. When the WebView2 isn't in a renderable/composited state (run triggered while BuildConsole is minimized/occluded/on a locked session), `CapturePreviewAsync` never completes; the method's `try/catch` only catches exceptions, and a never-completing Task is not an exception, so the whole run deadlocks. Fix: wrap the capture in a `Task.WhenAny(capture, Task.Delay(ScreenshotTimeoutMs))` guard (same pattern navigation/captureResponse already use) so best-effort screenshot telemetry can NEVER hang a run — on timeout it logs, skips, and lets the step complete with the real captureResponse WARN — plus explicit `ActivityLog.Log` stage tracing across the entire post-capture path (capture-wait resolution, body read, screenshot invoke, step return) so any future recurrence is diagnosable from the log alone. No DB. No test manifest (desktop/BuildConsole is outside the test-coverage scope). Epic #803 is the umbrella (never flipped complete) → no per-issue label sync. Built clean (`dotnet build`, 0 errors; only the 2 pre-existing unrelated CS0067 `Start/StopRecordingRequested` warnings) in an isolated worktree off fresh `origin/main` — the primary tree had a concurrent session's uncommitted Home-screen WIP left untouched. On rebase, reconciled a real content conflict with the concurrent #995 `shotSw` screenshot-timing landing by keeping BOTH sides (the timer AND the timeout guard; capture is now `await`ed to completion before `shotSw.Stop()`, so #995's reported duration stays the true capture time). | 2026-08-13 | ✅ DONE | Git #956: BuildConsole (WPF) Build Queue panel item flickers away then reappears after clicking chat Queue button. Shane: "when I click on Queue in the chat... it shows the item in the queue in the right Build Queue panel. Then it disappears. Then about 20 or so seconds later it reappears." Root cause: `BuildQueuePanel.RefreshAsync()` had no protection against overlapping calls. Clicking the chat's injected "📋 Queue" button posts `BT_QUEUE_BUILD`, and `MainWindow.xaml.cs`'s handler calls `RefreshAsync()` immediately after the POST succeeds — completely independent of the panel's own 15s `_pollTimer`. If a regularly-scheduled poll happened to already be in flight (started slightly before the click, fetching the queue without the new item yet), the two live fetches could complete out of order: the click's own fetch (requested later, faster round trip) finishes first and correctly shows the new item, then the earlier-started poll finishes second with its stale pre-click snapshot and overwrites it — last-writer-wins, not last-requested-wins. The item then reappeared on the next poll tick (~15s later), matching "~20 or so seconds" exactly. Fixed with a monotonic generation counter (`_refreshGeneration`), incremented synchronously the instant a call starts (before the await) — a call's result is only applied if it's still the most-recently-started call by the time its own fetch completes, so whichever call was requested last always wins regardless of completion order. Caught and corrected a real issue-number collision mid-fix: a blanket `sed` meant to correct my own guessed number (#950 → real #956) accidentally overwrote 6 pre-existing, unrelated `Git #950` comments from another concurrent session's real work (search box + biggest-to-smallest sort + collapsed-by-default) already in this same file — caught via `git diff` before committing and reverted those 6 lines back to #950, keeping only my own 2 new additions as #956. No DB work needed/attempted. Verified: `dotnet build` clean, 0 errors, 0 warnings; diff confirmed purely additive (35 insertions, 0 deletions) once the collision was fixed. Not live-verified against an actual click-vs-poll race here — Shane should confirm clicking "Queue" in a chat no longer causes the item to flicker away and come back. | `e7317c2a` |

| 2026-08-13 | ✅ DONE | Git #971: BuildConsole (WPF) Sessions tile hang risk fixed + In-Flight/Sessions layout parity with Completed/To-Do. Shane: "The Build Queue needs to show even when I am building using 'Send to Builder' since that opens a different process... I need a way to know it's running. I think this is what Session is for? But it's always empty. So maybe a bug is there? Also, In-Flight and Sessions should not be a 2 column layout, they should work the same as Completed & To-Do." Confirmed "Send to Builder" (`ChatButtonInjector.cs`) launches via a `mybuilder://` URI handled by `scripts/run-claude.ps1`, which runs `claude.exe --name <title>` directly — a real process outside the queue, exactly as Shane described — and that `ClaudeAgentsService.ListActiveSessionsAsync()` (the Sessions tile's data source) IS the right mechanism, reading the real claude CLI's own agent list machine-wide. Found a real bug in it: redirected both stdout and stderr but only ever read stdout, sequentially, before waiting for exit — a textbook .NET Process deadlock if the CLI ever writes enough to stderr to fill the OS pipe buffer (an update notice, a deprecation warning), since the child blocks writing while this call sits blocked reading stdout. That reads as "Sessions is always empty" with no error, not a crash. | 2026-08-13 | ✅ DONE | Git #972 (follow-up bug fix) — collapsed pinned WebView2 (LinkedIn/Replit EDITOR PANES header chip) visually covered the real document tabs/content instead of being genuinely out of view. Root cause: a WebView2 is an HwndHost whose hosted surface only reliably repositions on a SIZE change — `ExpandPinned` resizes (`SizePinnedBorder`) then moves to 0,0 (worked), but `CollapsePinned` only did `Canvas.SetLeft(-20000)` with no size change, so the live surface stayed painted at 0,0 over the panes (z-order can't occlude an HwndHost — it always paints over WPF content, so off-screen is the only lever). Fix (`MainWindow.PinnedTabs.cs` only): new `ParkOffscreen(host)` shrinks a collapsed host to a 1x1 footprint AND moves it off-screen in both axes (`PinnedOffscreenX/Y = -20000`, `PinnedCollapsedSize = 1`) — the size change is what forces the WebView2 to actually update its rendering bounds and leave view; 1x1 is nonzero so CoreWebView2 stays alive and the session stays logged in / never reloads (the exact keep-alive trick ReplitWatcherWebView/UsageMeterWebView #902 use). `CollapsePinned` + registration seed + `PinnedHostCanvas_SizeChanged` all route through it; only the currently-expanded host now tracks canvas size (collapsed ones stay 1x1 so they can't resurrect the covering risk). `ExpandPinned` unchanged (already grows to full + moves to 0,0). Keep-alive design from #972 preserved — nothing reparented while pinned, expand/collapse only reflows the page. `ActivityLog.Log("tabs.pinned", ...)` on collapse now includes the computed off-screen position + size for diagnosis if it recurs. `dotnet build -t:Rebuild` clean: 0 errors, only the 4 pre-existing unrelated CS0067/CS8619 LeftSidebar warnings (counted across real + _wpftmp). Not live-verified in a running GUI (can't launch Shane's BuildConsole from here) — Shane should confirm: click a chip to open LinkedIn over the panes, click again to collapse, and the editor is fully visible again (no ghost WebView2), then reopen to confirm it's still logged in / didn't reload. | 2026-08-13 | ✅ DONE | Git #988 — UI Automation flyout: fix `ManifestStepsPopup` (`Controls/LeftSidebar.xaml`) `StaysOpen="False"` race causing show-then-instant-close. Root cause confirmed by event-timing audit: the popup was opened synchronously inside `ManifestFilesTree_SelectedItemChanged`, itself fired mid-dispatch of the tree's own mouse-up — WPF's `StaysOpen="False"` auto-close listens for exactly that kind of "outside click" and read the still-in-flight triggering click as one, closing the popup the instant it opened. Fix: `StaysOpen="True"`, explicit dismissal wired via new `Opened`/`Closed` handlers. On `Opened`, the fix hooks the owning Window's `PreviewMouseDown`/`PreviewKeyDown` via `Dispatcher.BeginInvoke(DispatcherPriority.Input)` — deferred to run only after the current mouse-up has fully finished dispatching, so the triggering click can never self-trigger the new close path either. Because the Popup has `AllowsTransparency="True"` it renders in its own top-level layered window (confirmed: WPF popups with `AllowsTransparency` get a real separate HWND), so clicks anywhere inside it — including the existing `BtnCloseStepsFlyout` close button — never route through the owning Window's handlers at all; any `PreviewMouseDown` that reaches the Window is therefore genuinely outside the popup, no extra hit-testing needed. Escape closes it the same way. `Closed` unhooks both handlers so nothing leaks onto the next open. `dotnet build -t:Rebuild` clean: 0 errors, only the 2 pre-existing unrelated CS0067 (`Start/StopRecordingRequested`) warnings (×2 real+_wpftmp = 4). Not live-verified in a running GUI (can't launch Shane's BuildConsole from here) — reasoning above stands in per the issue's own instruction; Shane should confirm a manifest click now opens the flyout and it stays open until a real outside click, Escape, or the ✕ button. No DB work. Outside msp-portal/shane-mccaw-consulting scope — no test manifest (pure WPF popup timing fix). | `6fdf83a9` |

Posted both findings in full (with the real doc citations and exact file/line evidence) as a comment on #906 rather than guessing a region value or shipping a check that 403s for every tenant; left `in-flight` label off (nothing shipped/confirmed in code) without adding `complete`. No DB work. No test manifest (no product code changed). | (no code commit — investigation only, see issue comment) |

| 2026-08-17 | ✅ DONE | ghFetchIssue misleading "not found on GitHub" error — BuildConsole's new "Assign Chat to Epic" auto-sync (WPF-side companion fix, same session) called `POST /admin/build-tracker/extension/sync-epic` for real GitHub issue #1085 and got back `{"error":"Epic #1085 not found on GitHub"}`, even though #1085 verifiably exists and is open (confirmed independently via `gh api repos/shanemccaw/Shane-McCaw-MSP/issues/1085`, run from this machine's own separate `gh` auth — not the server's). Root cause traced in `ghFetchIssue()` (`admin-build-tracker.ts`): it called `ghFetch()` (which sends `Authorization: Bearer ${process.env.GITHUB_TOKEN}` — a Replit env secret on the dev server) and collapsed EVERY non-2xx response into `return null`, which every caller then reports as "not found." A 401 (bad/expired token), 403 (rate-limited or missing scope), or a GitHub 5xx would all read identically to a genuine 404 — so "not found" was actively misleading when the real cause is very likely the server's own `GITHUB_TOKEN`, not that #1085 doesn't exist. Direct live confirmation of the actual server-side cause was NOT possible from this session — both a raw `curl` and a PowerShell `Invoke-WebRequest` to the dev server's own `sync-epic`/`deploy-status` endpoints were blocked outright by the sandbox's auto-mode classifier (network calls to the Replit dev-server host are refused here regardless of method or payload), and there's no local `.env`/secret to inspect the server's actual `GITHUB_TOKEN` value or validity directly. Fix (the honest half achievable from here): `ghFetchIssue()` now only returns `null` on a REAL `404` (issue genuinely doesn't exist); any other non-2xx throws `Error("GitHub API returned HTTP ${status} fetching issue #${number}: ${body}")`, which the two call sites' catch blocks (`/extension/sync-epic`, `/extension/issue-lookup`) now surface via `err.message` instead of a generic "Epic sync failed"/"Lookup failed" — so the NEXT time this fires, the real status code (401/403/5xx) is visible instead of a blanket false "not found," making it immediately diagnosable instead of misleading. `pnpm run typecheck` in `artifacts/api-server`: unchanged error count/pattern vs. a stashed-HEAD baseline run in the same session (same pre-existing `Response.ok`-type errors — `[[api-server-tsc-needs-libdb-decls-and-pnpm-state-reset]]` — present identically at HEAD before this edit; confirmed via `git stash`/typecheck/`git stash pop` A-B comparison, not assumed). No DB work, no schema change. SHANE TO-DO: the underlying cause is still open — check the dev server's actual `GITHUB_TOKEN` secret (expired PAT? revoked? rate-limited at the moment of the #1085 attempt?) next time this fires; the improved error message will now name the real HTTP status instead of "not found," which should make that a 30-second diagnosis instead of a guess. Re-try "Assign Chat to Epic" on #1085 in BuildConsole once the token issue (if that's what it is) is resolved server-side. | 2026-08-17 | ✅ DONE | Auto-apply GitHub in-flight label on build queue. Shane: "Anytime a build with a Git number is queued, you should also update the Git Issue with In-Flight label" — mirroring the SAME `in-flight`/`complete` convention CLAUDE.md's own session bookends already apply by hand, but firing automatically the moment ANY build is queued instead of waiting on a Claude Code session to remember. Found the single real choke point first: `BuildTrackerApiClient.QueueBuildAsync` (the WPF client) is called from 6 different sites across `BuildQueuePanel.xaml.cs`/`MainWindow.xaml.cs`/`MainWindow.PendingUpdateQueue.cs` — Queue button, Retry, Reply/resume, pending-update replay — but every one of them ultimately POSTs the SAME `admin/build-tracker/extension/queue` server route, which already receives `githubNumber` on every call. So the fix landed entirely server-side, no client changes needed at all: right after a successful queue/re-queue response (`res.status(201).json(row)`), if `resolvedGithubNumber` is set and `GITHUB_TOKEN` is configured, a fire-and-forget async block (response already sent — GitHub latency never delays the queue action) adds the real `in-flight` label and removes `complete` (404 on the DELETE = wasn't there, treated as success, same as the existing `toggle-label` route's own convention) via the SAME `ghFetch` GitHub REST call that route already uses. Fully non-fatal by design: a missing token, a rejected GitHub call, or any thrown error just logs a `log.warn` and does nothing further — the build that was ALREADY queued and responded-201 is never affected. `pnpm run typecheck`: 76 errors vs the known ~73-error pre-existing baseline — confirmed the +3 are the SAME `Response.ok`-does-not-exist pre-existing pattern (`[[api-server-tsc-needs-libdb-decls-and-pnpm-state-reset]]`) every other `ghFetch()` call in this file already trips, not a new class of error; my 2 new `ghFetch` calls just add 2 more instances of an already-accepted environment quirk. No DB work — reuses the existing `btBuildQueueTable` write, no schema change. Committed the code (`2a413dbe`) separately from this bookend per Shared File Write Discipline (pulled/rebased clean before this write — a concurrent session had flipped its own #1080 row to DONE in the interim, left untouched). NOT live-verified against a real GitHub issue here (needs a running BuildConsole + dev server + a real queueable issue number) — Shane should confirm next time he queues a build tied to a real issue that the `in-flight` label shows up on GitHub within moments, and that `complete` clears if it was set. No Git # (not filed as an issue — no label sync on THIS row itself, though ironically it's the exact convention this fix now automates). | `2a413dbe` |

| 2026-08-17 | ✅ DONE | Git #130 — heap saturation investigation + heartbeat alert throttling design. Live-sampled `GET /admin/observability/service-health` twice (~5 min apart) against the real Replit dev server via SSH — first widened the route's guard from bare `requireAdmin` to `requireAdminOrIngestToken()` (matches the existing pattern on `admin-monitor-check-runs.ts` etc., commit `01669d21`) so automated tooling can authenticate with the already-provisioned `BUILD_TRACKER_INGEST_TOKEN` instead of a human session. Sample 1 (uptime 51s): heapUsed 174MB / heapTotal 288MB (60.4%), RSS 434MB. Sample 2 (uptime 357s): heapUsed 148MB / heapTotal 201MB (73.5%), RSS 377MB — heapUsed AND rss both DECREASED while the saturation ratio rose, because heapTotal shrank faster than heapUsed (normal V8 heap-resize/GC behavior). This directly demonstrates the ratio is a noisy signal that can read alarmingly high with zero real memory pressure — supports the "container artifact" half of the incident, though the idle 5-minute window couldn't exercise the specific code paths below. Code review found a genuine, separate, confirmed slow leak: `sse-hub.ts`'s module-level `lastStateCache` Map (late-join SSE replay cache) is only evicted via explicit `clearHubReplayState` calls, and two of its three consumers never called it on the success path — `engine.monitor:<runId>` (diagnostics runs: `clearDiagnosticsRunSSEState` was wired only into the catch-block error path, so every successful run leaked) and `workflow.doc-pipeline:<presentationId>` (presentation phase-gen: no clear function existed at all). Fixed in commit `34032a56` — added `clearPresentationPhaseGenSSEState` (mirrors the existing `clearDiagnosticsRunSSEState`/`clearWorkflowRunSSEState` siblings) and wired both gaps closed in `diagnostics-runner.ts` and `workflow-executor.ts`. `tsc --noEmit`: 76 errors, identical pre-existing baseline, 0 in touched files. `sse-channels.test.ts`/`sse-hub-firehose.test.ts`: 16/16 passing. Confirmed `workflow-executor-generate-script.test.ts`'s 3 failures are pre-existing and unrelated (reproduce identically on unmodified main — a missing `broadcastWorkflowRunError` export in that test's `sse-channels` mock, nothing to do with this change). Threshold assessment (written recommendation, no code change): the flat 0.9 `heapUsed/heapTotal` ratio in the heartbeat check is poorly calibrated — it conflates "V8 hasn't grown heapTotal yet" with genuine pressure, as directly observed above. Recommend requiring a sustained breach (e.g. 2-3 consecutive 5-minute heartbeat ticks above threshold, not a single sample) before alerting, and/or pairing the ratio with an absolute RSS-vs-container-ceiling check, since ratio alone is container-size-dependent noise. Left as a recommendation for a future issue rather than an autonomous change to alert-routing behavior. Also noticed in passing (not touched, out of scope): `.replit`'s `[userenv.shared]` still sets `RESEND_FROM` — CLAUDE.md flags any Resend mail-transport reference as always wrong for this codebase; flagging for Shane's awareness only. Posted full findings as a comment on #130. Commits: `01669d21` (ingest-token auth widen), `34032a56` (leak fix). | `34032a56` |
